2026-10-18T04:27:37.9426834Z   QWEN_COMMIT_MESSAGE: [require-build] [cluster:firmus] [nodes:2] [profile] Build and serve the Firmus max bundle once as the fastest configuration derived from the worker evidence

Why: the user asked to derive the fastest configuration by reasoning and
use the GPU only to confirm it, not to isolate effects. This is that one
confirmation: a fresh image with every adopted change, served once with
the official 60/120/30 client plus the separate profile pass so the
result can be attributed without further jobs. The consolidated kernel
bench (39096856098) was cancelled before reaching Slurm for the same
reason; the serving job's own numerical gates (check-firmus-megamoe.py,
check-firmus-decode-kernels.py) run before the server starts.

Configuration and the reasoning for each choice (plan:
research/runtime-performance/2026-10-18-firmus-maximum-performance-plan.md,
integration: research/runtime-performance/2026-10-18-firmus-max-bundle.md):

DeepGEMM MegaMoE, full 14-patch series (header ff7a58cc):
- dispatch-drop-l1-weight (komoriyuta): removes an unused remote load;
  measured faster alone, Prefill spill 40 -> 20 B.
- prefill-tail (dynamic FC2 claiming + paired-load combine): -5 to -6%
  at 1024/2048 tokens, -2 to -5% (p10) at 4096, bit-identical, two runs.
- prefill-mainloop-prefetch: moves the next K block's scale loads and
  reciprocals off the WGMMA critical path with the same products in the
  same order; strictly fewer instructions between WGMMA groups, 96 regs.
- combine-parallel (komoriyuta) + decode-dynamic-schedule: Decode-128
  203.0 -> 196-197 us, Decode-4..32 -4 to -5%, bit-identical, two runs.
- decode-l1-store-wait: only the storing warp waits for its TMA store;
  same data, fewer CTA-wide waits.
Every patch is either measured faster alone or removes work from a
critical path without adding any, so the combination is expected to be
no slower than its best subset; the combined compile is Prefill 96 regs
44/60 B spill, Decode 168 regs 0 spill. Not included: flat NVLink
barriers (protocol change in every geometry, never run on a GPU).

SGLang (15 existing + 8 new patches) and router:
- komoriyuta's FP8 KV write fusion and padded Top-K (fixed by him after
  39077653567/39087648892), token-warp QK-norm/RoPE (141 us/layer
  Prefill was ~4x off bandwidth), fused add-RMSNorm + group-128 FP8 quant
  for the QKV input (the FP8 QKV input already exists; ~115 MB instead of
  ~181 MB of traffic per Prefill layer). Estimated Prefill -11 to -12 ms
  per step, Decode ~-1 ms per step.
- Request-aligned Prefill chunks, optimistic prefill 2: shorter handoff
  (TTFT) with the same supply.
- Decode async admission: removes the 25-31 ms CPU stalls from blocking
  copies on the WAR-held schedule stream (227 waits enumerated in the A1
  profile); modelled Decode step -8%. Decode polling interval back to 1.
- DP tie rotation and 256-row idle Prefill ranks: removes the SwapAB
  MegaMoE calls on idle ranks (3.3 ms/layer), modelled Prefill -1 to -3.5%.
- Router TCP_NODELAY: removes the fixed ~41 ms router-to-client delay.
No change lowers precision: FP8 weights/KV/activations stay where they
were, nothing new is cast to FP8, nothing is skipped; differences are
FP summation order/FMA only (task rules Q1/Q3/Q4).

Decode cap 136 (34/rank, graph bs 34) and concurrency 192: with the
expected Decode step reduction (async admission, MegaMoE, small kernels,
together roughly -10%) and Prefill supply rising to ~43-44 req/s, the
Decode running count needed is ~125-131, inside 136, while 136 also stays
under the ITL limit if the Decode gains fail to appear (W1 model: p99
19.5 ms at today's step). c192 keeps the Prefill queue fed at ~4.2 s per
request; a few extra requests cost ~0.1% while starving costs percents.

This commit itself only removes the kernel-bench staging directories and
installs the timeline patch rebased onto the bundle header ff7a58cc (it
applies there; verified), so the kernel bench keeps working once this
image is the reference.

Validation: pytest (qwen-experiment-metrics/tests) and ruff pass; the
SGLang chain applies on fc275674 and every source check passes; the
DeepGEMM series reproduces header ff7a58cc from v0.1.5.
